============================ SYSTEM PROMPT ===========================


You are an autonomous research scientist, and have been tasked with creating and carrying out a plan of research.

I'll provide you a top-level Research Task (e.g., "Characterize how well the language model Llama can perform 2-digit addition.").

First, decide on the appropriate strategy for performing the task, either plan (for more complex tasks) or simply do it (for simpler tasks).

Then:
 - For a 'do' strategy, you'll generate the Python code and execute it. This strategy essentially treats the task as a 1-step plan (so no planning step needed).
 - For a 'plan' strategy, you'll:
     1. Generate a plan
     2. Execute the plan, by iteratively working through each step. At each step, you generate Python code to perform the step, then I will execute the code for you and show you the results, then you'll reflect on what do to next.

Here are details on each of those phases:

======================================================================
	PHASE 1. PICK A STRATEGY
======================================================================

You'll be asked to reflect on the immediate task, and decide on the appropriate strategy to follow, one of:
 - do: a task you understand and can do directly in Python, with one or a few commands
 - plan: a larger but well-defined task, that you can generate a plan for (then subsequently follow)

Answer with a JSON structure of the form: {"strategy":STRATEGY, "explanation": EXPLANATION}, where 
 - STRATEGY is either "do" or "plan"
 - EXPLANATION is a natural-language explanation

Some examples:

Task: What is 1 + 1?
{"strategy":"do", "explanation":"A single line of Python will answer this request, so no planning needed."}

Task: How good is Llama at science?
{"strategy":"plan", "explanation":"This involves multiple steps, so planning is needed."}

======================================================================
	PHASE 2. GENERATING A PLAN (for the 'plan' strategy)
======================================================================

 - Each plan step should be expressed in natural language.
 - Each plan step should be implementable in Python (done at a later sage).

When generating a plan, return the plan as a JSON object with the following structure:
     {"plan": [{"step_number":1, "step":DESCRIPTION}, {"step_number":2, "step":DESCRIPTION}, ....]}

======================================================================
	PHASE 3. EXECUTING A PLAN (for the 'plan' strategy)
======================================================================

1. Overview

Given a plan, we will then interact to repeatedly execute an ACTION then REFLECTION cycle to perform each step in the plan in turn.

In the ACTION step, you'll generate some Python code to perform the next step in the plan. I'll then execute the Python code and show you the results.

In the REFLECTION step, you'll reflect on whether the code execution was successful, and what to do next. Your possible next steps can include redoing the entire plan itself, or stopping if the research task is complete.

2. The Action step

I will give you the next step to perform, and you'll return an implementation of that step in Python, along with a "thought" describing your reasoning. In this step, you should return a JSTON object of the form:

      {"thought":THOUGHT, "action":PYTHON_CODE}

I'll then execute the code and add the results at the end of the conversation, then ask you to reflect on the results.
Several examples are given below.

Useful Python Functions:

Here are two built-in Python functions that might be useful for you:

(a) Basic Calls to a LLM

def call_llm(prompt=prompt:str, model=model:str, temperature=temperature:float=0):
Purpose:
    Basic access to an LLM.
Args:
    prompt (str): The question/instruction to give to the LLM.
    model (str): The full model name of a GPT or LiteLLM model, e.g., 'gpt-5', 'gpt-4.1-nano', 'claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929', 'together_ai/meta-llama/Meta-Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct-Turbo'
    temperature (int): The temperature to use during LLM generation (default 0)
Returns:
    response (str): The LLM response.
Example:
    call_llm(prompt="Generate a new research idea about large language models.")
->  Title: Investigating the Impact of Multimodal Inputs on Large Language ....

(b) Writing a Report

def write_report():
Purpose:
    Writes a report to a file, and also prints it out as a text file in stdout.
    The report summarizes the research performed in the current dialog history.
    IMPORTANT: Make sure that all the information (examples etc.) required for the report is visible in the conversational history, as write_report() turns that history into a report.
    If it is not, first print out the required information (examples, tables, etc.)
Args:
    None
Returns:
    The path stem to the reports. Add the suffixes ".txt" or ".html" to the stem for the path to the plain text / HTML report file, respectively.
	
Example:
    write_report()
->  Reports written to:
    - c:/Users/peter/Desktop/panda/output/experiment-20250705-213812/experiment.html
    - c:/Users/peter/Desktop/panda/output/experiment-20250705-213812/experiment.txt
    # and returns the stem "c:/Users/peter/Desktop/panda/output/experiment-20250705-213812/experiment"

3. The Reflection Step

In the reflection step, you'll reflect on progress so far, to help decide what to do next. You will assess, among other things, whether the task is complete, whether the current step is complete, and whether a software (Python) error occured when executing code.

Here is some background_knowledge for the research you are about to do:
Background Knowledge for Step 3: H6 - Predictive Self-Correction with Phase-Modulated Consolidation (PSC)

== MOTIVATION ==
Our previous experiment (Step 2) showed that using forward weights W for backward credit propagation (OPCA) FAILS — performing worse than random feedback alignment. The core problem: backward propagation through ANY matrix (W, W^T, or random B) is an inherently non-local, non-biological operation.

NEW APPROACH: Instead of propagating error backward, have each neuron PREDICT its own future contribution to error using only FORWARD dynamics and LOCAL information. The oscillatory phase then gates when these predictions are evaluated and consolidated.

== ALGORITHM: PSC (Predictive Self-Correction) ==

Each neuron maintains:
1. A "prediction compartment" p_i(t) that predicts the neuron's own future error contribution
2. An "eligibility trace" e_ij(t) marking recent synaptic co-activity
3. A "consolidation flag" that activates during specific oscillatory phases

The key insight: A neuron's future contribution to output error is correlated with how much its activity deviates from what would be "expected" given the current input context. This deviation can be computed LOCALLY.

== FORMAL DEFINITION ==

Forward dynamics (same as standard RNN):
  a_t = W_rec * h_{t-1} + W_in * x_t + b
  h_t = tanh(a_t)
  y_t = W_out * h_t + b_out

Prediction compartment (runs in parallel, LOCAL computation):
  Each neuron i has auxiliary variable p_i tracking "predicted activity":
  p_i(t) = (1-beta) * p_i(t-1) + beta * [W_pred_row_i * h(t-1)]

  where W_pred is a LOCAL prediction weight (each neuron predicts itself from neighbors)
  The prediction error: delta_pred_i(t) = h_i(t) - p_i(t)

  This prediction error is a LOCAL signal — it tells neuron i "how surprising is my current activity given recent history?"

Oscillatory modulation:
  phi(t) = sin(2*pi*t/T_theta)  [theta oscillation]

  Consolidation window: when phi(t) > threshold (e.g., > 0.5), plasticity is enabled
  Outside consolidation window: only eligibility traces accumulate

Learning rule:
  e_ij(t) = lambda * e_ij(t-1) + h_j(t-1) * dtanh_i(t)  [eligibility trace, decaying]

  At output time steps (when loss is available):
    output_error_i = (y_i - target_i) * W_out_ij  [projected output error, for output neurons only]

  At all time steps, the "credit signal" for neuron i is:
    s_i(t) = gamma * |delta_pred_i(t)| * sign(delta_pred_i(t)) + (1-gamma) * output_error_i(t)

    where output_error_i(t) = 0 for non-output time steps (or during delay period)
    and gamma controls the balance between predictive and output-driven learning

  Weight update (only during consolidation window):
    Delta W_ij += -lr * s_i(t) * e_ij(t) * gate(phi(t))

    where gate(phi(t)) = max(0, sin(2*pi*t/T_theta))

  Prediction weight update (always active, self-supervised):
    Delta W_pred_ij += -lr_pred * delta_pred_i(t) * h_j(t-1)  [standard Hebbian prediction learning]

== WHY THIS MIGHT WORK ==

1. NO BACKWARD PROPAGATION: Credit is assigned via forward predictions, not backward error flow
2. LOCALITY: Each neuron only uses its own activity, its prediction, and its eligibility trace
3. OSCILLATORY GATING: Provides temporal structure to consolidation (biologically grounded in theta)
4. SELF-SUPERVISED PREDICTION: The prediction compartment learns WITHOUT external error signals
5. OUTPUT ERROR as ANCHOR: At output time steps, actual error is available and anchors the learning

The biological story:
- Prediction compartment = apical dendrite computing top-down expectation
- Prediction error = dendritic calcium signal (surprise)
- Eligibility trace = synaptic tag (calcium/CaMKII dependent)
- Oscillatory gate = theta-dependent plasticity window (hippocampal LTP is theta-gated)
- Consolidation = synaptic tagging and capture during specific oscillatory phases

== WHAT MAKES THIS GENUINELY NOVEL ==

1. Prior predictive coding work predicts INPUTS to a layer from above. We predict a neuron's OWN activity from its LATERAL connections within the same recurrent layer.
2. Prior oscillatory work separates encoding/retrieval (Hasselmo SPEAR). We separate prediction-evaluation/consolidation.
3. The combination: "local lateral self-prediction" + "oscillatory consolidation gating" + "eligibility-trace credit" has not been proposed.
4. It's NOT just feedback alignment + oscillations (which we tested and it barely helps).
5. It's NOT just predictive coding in time (which uses backward prediction errors across layers).

== EXPERIMENT ==

Test PSC on the same copy task as before for direct comparison:
- Copy task: 8 bits, 10-step delay
- Vanilla RNN, hidden size 128
- Compare: BPTT, PSC (our new method), FA (baseline), OPCA (previous attempt)
- Training: 5000 iterations, batch size 32
- Hyperparameter search:
  - Learning rate: {0.001, 0.005, 0.01}
  - beta (prediction decay): {0.1, 0.3, 0.5}
  - gamma (prediction vs output balance): {0.3, 0.5, 0.7}
  - lambda (eligibility decay): {0.9, 0.95}
  - T_theta (oscillation period): {10, 20}

Also test: PSC WITHOUT oscillatory gating (always consolidate) to isolate the contribution of oscillatory modulation.

ENVIRONMENT: conda environment `panda`, PyTorch
WORKING DIRECTORY: /allen/programs/mindscope/workgroups/auto-model/zihan.zhang/ai2/asta-test/run_ann_learning_rule/run3/


NOW: Here is your top-level research task:
Implement and test the PSC (Predictive Self-Correction) learning rule for RNNs on the copy task.

ALGORITHM:
PSC avoids backward propagation entirely. Each neuron has:
1. Main dynamics: h_t = tanh(W_rec * h_{t-1} + W_in * x_t + b)
2. Prediction compartment: p_i(t) = (1-beta)*p_i(t-1) + beta*(W_pred[i,:] @ h(t-1))
   This predicts the neuron's own activity from lateral connections.
3. Prediction error: delta_pred_i(t) = h_i(t) - p_i(t)
4. Eligibility trace: e_ij(t) = lambda*e_ij(t-1) + h_j(t-1)*dtanh_i(t)
5. Output error (only at output timesteps): err_i(t) = (y - target) projected back via W_out^T to hidden
6. Credit signal: s_i(t) = gamma*delta_pred_i(t) + (1-gamma)*output_err_i(t)
   (output_err_i is 0 except during output timesteps)
7. Oscillatory gate: gate(t) = max(0, sin(2*pi*t/T_theta))
8. Weight update: Delta_W_rec_ij = -lr * s_i(t) * e_ij(t) * gate(t)
9. Prediction weight update: Delta_W_pred_ij = -lr_pred * delta_pred_i(t) * h_j(t-1)

Use Adam momentum for weight updates.

EXPERIMENT:
- Task: Copy task — input 8 binary values, wait 10 blank steps, reproduce 8 values
- Architecture: Vanilla RNN, hidden size 128  
- Compare 4 methods:
  (a) BPTT (gold standard)
  (b) PSC with oscillatory gating (our method)
  (c) PSC WITHOUT oscillatory gating (ablation: always consolidate, gate=1 always)
  (d) FA (feedback alignment baseline, from previous experiments)
- Training: 5000 iterations, batch size 32
- PSC hyperparameter grid search (pick best per method):
  - lr: {0.001, 0.005, 0.01}
  - beta (prediction decay): {0.1, 0.3}
  - gamma (prediction vs output weight): {0.3, 0.5, 0.7}  
  - lambda (eligibility decay): {0.9, 0.95}
  - T_theta (oscillation period in timesteps): {10, 20}
  - lr_pred (prediction learning rate): 0.01
- For the grid search: do a quick 1000-iter run for each combo, pick top 3 configs, then run full 5000 iters
- Metrics: Final MSE, bit accuracy, convergence speed

IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION NOTES:
- W_pred is a SEPARATE weight matrix (128x128) for prediction, initialized randomly
- W_pred is learned via simple Hebbian prediction error rule (self-supervised)
- The main W_rec update uses the CREDIT SIGNAL (s_i) not the raw prediction error
- Output weights W_out are updated using standard (output_error * h_t) regardless of method
- Use grad clipping (norm 1.0) for stability

ENVIRONMENT: Use conda environment panda. PyTorch.
Save results as JSON.

============================== Panda ==============================

Doable: yes
Explanation: The entire plan is implementable in Python using PyTorch and standard scientific libraries. All steps involve: (1) implementing algorithms from scratch in PyTorch (PSC, BPTT, FA), (2) running experiments programmatically with no human interaction needed, (3) performing hyperparameter grid search via nested loops, (4) computing metrics and saving to JSON. No web access, human labeling, or pre-training of large models is required. The RNN training is from scratch on a simple synthetic task (copy task), which is fast and feasible within the compute environment described.

----------------------------------------

Top-Level Task: Implement and test the PSC (Predictive Self-Correction) learning rule for RNNs on the copy task.

ALGORITHM:
PSC avoids backward propagation entirely. Each neuron has:
1. Main dynamics: h_t = tanh(W_rec * h_{t-1} + W_in * x_t + b)
2. Prediction compartment: p_i(t) = (1-beta)*p_i(t-1) + beta*(W_pred[i,:] @ h(t-1))
   This predicts the neuron's own activity from lateral connections.
3. Prediction error: delta_pred_i(t) = h_i(t) - p_i(t)
4. Eligibility trace: e_ij(t) = lambda*e_ij(t-1) + h_j(t-1)*dtanh_i(t)
5. Output error (only at output timesteps): err_i(t) = (y - target) projected back via W_out^T to hidden
6. Credit signal: s_i(t) = gamma*delta_pred_i(t) + (1-gamma)*output_err_i(t)
   (output_err_i is 0 except during output timesteps)
7. Oscillatory gate: gate(t) = max(0, sin(2*pi*t/T_theta))
8. Weight update: Delta_W_rec_ij = -lr * s_i(t) * e_ij(t) * gate(t)
9. Prediction weight update: Delta_W_pred_ij = -lr_pred * delta_pred_i(t) * h_j(t-1)

Use Adam momentum for weight updates.

EXPERIMENT:
- Task: Copy task — input 8 binary values, wait 10 blank steps, reproduce 8 values
- Architecture: Vanilla RNN, hidden size 128  
- Compare 4 methods:
  (a) BPTT (gold standard)
  (b) PSC with oscillatory gating (our method)
  (c) PSC WITHOUT oscillatory gating (ablation: always consolidate, gate=1 always)
  (d) FA (feedback alignment baseline, from previous experiments)
- Training: 5000 iterations, batch size 32
- PSC hyperparameter grid search (pick best per method):
  - lr: {0.001, 0.005, 0.01}
  - beta (prediction decay): {0.1, 0.3}
  - gamma (prediction vs output weight): {0.3, 0.5, 0.7}  
  - lambda (eligibility decay): {0.9, 0.95}
  - T_theta (oscillation period in timesteps): {10, 20}
  - lr_pred (prediction learning rate): 0.01
- For the grid search: do a quick 1000-iter run for each combo, pick top 3 configs, then run full 5000 iters
- Metrics: Final MSE, bit accuracy, convergence speed

IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION NOTES:
- W_pred is a SEPARATE weight matrix (128x128) for prediction, initialized randomly
- W_pred is learned via simple Hebbian prediction error rule (self-supervised)
- The main W_rec update uses the CREDIT SIGNAL (s_i) not the raw prediction error
- Output weights W_out are updated using standard (output_error * h_t) regardless of method
- Use grad clipping (norm 1.0) for stability

ENVIRONMENT: Use conda environment panda. PyTorch.
Save results as JSON.
Current Plan:
   1. Set up the environment: import necessary libraries (PyTorch, numpy, json, etc.), set random seeds for reproducibility, and define the copy task data generator (8 bits, 10-step delay).
   2. Implement the BPTT baseline: standard RNN with backpropagation through time, trained with Adam optimizer on the copy task.
   3. Implement the FA (Feedback Alignment) baseline: RNN where backward pass uses fixed random feedback matrices instead of transposed forward weights.
   4. Implement the PSC learning rule: RNN with prediction compartment (W_pred), eligibility traces, oscillatory gating, credit signal combining prediction error and output error, and Adam momentum for weight updates.
   5. Run the hyperparameter grid search for PSC (both with and without oscillatory gating): 1000-iteration quick runs for all combinations of lr, beta, gamma, lambda, T_theta. Record final MSE for each configuration.
   6. Select the top 3 hyperparameter configurations for PSC-with-gating and PSC-without-gating based on the grid search results.
   7. Run full 5000-iteration training for: (a) BPTT, (b) best PSC with oscillatory gating, (c) best PSC without oscillatory gating, (d) FA baseline. Record MSE and bit accuracy at regular intervals.
   8. Analyze and visualize results: plot learning curves, compare final MSE and bit accuracy across methods, compute convergence speed metrics.
   9. Save all results to JSON and write a comprehensive report summarizing findings.

The copy task involves: presenting 8 binary values, then 10 blank steps, then the network must reproduce the 8 values. I'll also verify the setup works by generating a sample batch.

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import numpy as np
import json
import math
import os
import time
from itertools import product
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use('Agg')
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Set random seeds for reproducibility
SEED = 42
torch.manual_seed(SEED)
np.random.seed(SEED)

# Device
device = torch.device('cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu')
print(f'Using device: {device}')

# Working directory
WORK_DIR = '/allen/programs/mindscope/workgroups/auto-model/zihan.zhang/ai2/asta-test/run_ann_learning_rule/run3/'
os.makedirs(WORK_DIR, exist_ok=True)
print(f'Working directory: {WORK_DIR}')

# ============================================================
# COPY TASK DATA GENERATOR
# ============================================================
# Task: present 8 binary bits, then 10 blank steps, then reproduce 8 bits
# Input dimension: 8 bits + 1 marker bit = 9 (marker indicates recall phase)
# Output dimension: 8 bits

N_BITS = 8
DELAY = 10
INPUT_DIM = N_BITS + 1  # +1 for the recall cue marker
OUTPUT_DIM = N_BITS
HIDDEN_SIZE = 128

# Sequence structure:
# t=0..7: input bits (marker=0)
# t=8..17: blank steps (marker=0 for first 9, marker=1 at t=17 to signal recall)
# t=18..25: output phase (input=zeros, marker=0) -- network must reproduce
# Total sequence length = 8 + 10 + 8 = 26

SEQ_LEN = N_BITS + DELAY + N_BITS  # = 26

def generate_copy_batch(batch_size, n_bits=N_BITS, delay=DELAY, device=device):
    """
    Generate a batch of copy task sequences.
    
    Returns:
        inputs: (batch_size, seq_len, input_dim) 
        targets: (batch_size, seq_len, output_dim) -- nonzero only at output timesteps
        output_mask: (batch_size, seq_len) -- 1 at output timesteps, 0 otherwise
    """
    seq_len = n_bits + delay + n_bits
    input_dim = n_bits + 1  # +1 for recall marker
    
    # Random binary patterns to copy
    patterns = torch.randint(0, 2, (batch_size, n_bits), dtype=torch.float32)
    
    inputs = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, input_dim)
    targets = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, n_bits)
    output_mask = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len)
    
    # Input phase: t = 0..n_bits-1, present the bits
    inputs[:, :n_bits, :n_bits] = patterns
    
    # Recall cue: at t = n_bits + delay - 1, set marker bit to 1
    inputs[:, n_bits + delay - 1, n_bits] = 1.0
    
    # Output phase: t = n_bits+delay .. seq_len-1, target = pattern
    output_start = n_bits + delay
    targets[:, output_start:, :] = patterns.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, n_bits, -1)
    output_mask[:, output_start:] = 1.0
    
    return inputs.to(device), targets.to(device), output_mask.to(device)

# Test the data generator
batch_size = 4
inputs, targets, output_mask = generate_copy_batch(batch_size)

print(f'\nCopy Task Configuration:')
print(f'  N_BITS: {N_BITS}')
print(f'  DELAY: {DELAY}')
print(f'  SEQ_LEN: {SEQ_LEN}')
print(f'  INPUT_DIM: {INPUT_DIM}')
print(f'  OUTPUT_DIM: {OUTPUT_DIM}')
print(f'  HIDDEN_SIZE: {HIDDEN_SIZE}')

print(f'\nSample batch shapes:')
print(f'  inputs: {inputs.shape}')
print(f'  targets: {targets.shape}')
print(f'  output_mask: {output_mask.shape}')

print(f'\nSample sequence (batch item 0):')
print(f'  Input bits at t=0..7: {inputs[0, :N_BITS, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()}')
print(f'  Recall marker at t=17: {inputs[0, N_BITS+DELAY-1, N_BITS].item()}')
print(f'  Target bits at t=18..25: {targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY:, :].cpu().numpy()}')
print(f'  Output mask: {output_mask[0].cpu().numpy()}')

# Verify targets match inputs
pattern_in = inputs[0, :N_BITS, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()
pattern_out = targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY:, :].cpu().numpy()
assert np.allclose(pattern_in, pattern_out), 'MISMATCH between input and target!'
print(f'\n Input/target consistency check passed')
print(f' Environment setup complete')

Step 1 not yet complete. Let's continue to work on it...

Top-Level Task: Implement and test the PSC (Predictive Self-Correction) learning rule for RNNs on the copy task.

ALGORITHM:
PSC avoids backward propagation entirely. Each neuron has:
1. Main dynamics: h_t = tanh(W_rec * h_{t-1} + W_in * x_t + b)
2. Prediction compartment: p_i(t) = (1-beta)*p_i(t-1) + beta*(W_pred[i,:] @ h(t-1))
   This predicts the neuron's own activity from lateral connections.
3. Prediction error: delta_pred_i(t) = h_i(t) - p_i(t)
4. Eligibility trace: e_ij(t) = lambda*e_ij(t-1) + h_j(t-1)*dtanh_i(t)
5. Output error (only at output timesteps): err_i(t) = (y - target) projected back via W_out^T to hidden
6. Credit signal: s_i(t) = gamma*delta_pred_i(t) + (1-gamma)*output_err_i(t)
   (output_err_i is 0 except during output timesteps)
7. Oscillatory gate: gate(t) = max(0, sin(2*pi*t/T_theta))
8. Weight update: Delta_W_rec_ij = -lr * s_i(t) * e_ij(t) * gate(t)
9. Prediction weight update: Delta_W_pred_ij = -lr_pred * delta_pred_i(t) * h_j(t-1)

Use Adam momentum for weight updates.

EXPERIMENT:
- Task: Copy task — input 8 binary values, wait 10 blank steps, reproduce 8 values
- Architecture: Vanilla RNN, hidden size 128  
- Compare 4 methods:
  (a) BPTT (gold standard)
  (b) PSC with oscillatory gating (our method)
  (c) PSC WITHOUT oscillatory gating (ablation: always consolidate, gate=1 always)
  (d) FA (feedback alignment baseline, from previous experiments)
- Training: 5000 iterations, batch size 32
- PSC hyperparameter grid search (pick best per method):
  - lr: {0.001, 0.005, 0.01}
  - beta (prediction decay): {0.1, 0.3}
  - gamma (prediction vs output weight): {0.3, 0.5, 0.7}  
  - lambda (eligibility decay): {0.9, 0.95}
  - T_theta (oscillation period in timesteps): {10, 20}
  - lr_pred (prediction learning rate): 0.01
- For the grid search: do a quick 1000-iter run for each combo, pick top 3 configs, then run full 5000 iters
- Metrics: Final MSE, bit accuracy, convergence speed

IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION NOTES:
- W_pred is a SEPARATE weight matrix (128x128) for prediction, initialized randomly
- W_pred is learned via simple Hebbian prediction error rule (self-supervised)
- The main W_rec update uses the CREDIT SIGNAL (s_i) not the raw prediction error
- Output weights W_out are updated using standard (output_error * h_t) regardless of method
- Use grad clipping (norm 1.0) for stability

ENVIRONMENT: Use conda environment panda. PyTorch.
Save results as JSON.
Current Plan:
   1. Set up the environment: import necessary libraries (PyTorch, numpy, json, etc.), set random seeds for reproducibility, and define the copy task data generator (8 bits, 10-step delay).
   2. Implement the BPTT baseline: standard RNN with backpropagation through time, trained with Adam optimizer on the copy task.
   3. Implement the FA (Feedback Alignment) baseline: RNN where backward pass uses fixed random feedback matrices instead of transposed forward weights.
   4. Implement the PSC learning rule: RNN with prediction compartment (W_pred), eligibility traces, oscillatory gating, credit signal combining prediction error and output error, and Adam momentum for weight updates.
   5. Run the hyperparameter grid search for PSC (both with and without oscillatory gating): 1000-iteration quick runs for all combinations of lr, beta, gamma, lambda, T_theta. Record final MSE for each configuration.
   6. Select the top 3 hyperparameter configurations for PSC-with-gating and PSC-without-gating based on the grid search results.
   7. Run full 5000-iteration training for: (a) BPTT, (b) best PSC with oscillatory gating, (c) best PSC without oscillatory gating, (d) FA baseline. Record MSE and bit accuracy at regular intervals.
   8. Analyze and visualize results: plot learning curves, compare final MSE and bit accuracy across methods, compute convergence speed metrics.
   9. Save all results to JSON and write a comprehensive report summarizing findings.

import subprocess

setup_code = '''
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import numpy as np
import json
import math
import os
import time
from itertools import product

# Set random seeds for reproducibility
SEED = 42
torch.manual_seed(SEED)
np.random.seed(SEED)

# Device
device = torch.device('cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu')
print(f'Using device: {device}')
if torch.cuda.is_available():
    print(f'GPU: {torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)}')

# Working directory
WORK_DIR = '/allen/programs/mindscope/workgroups/auto-model/zihan.zhang/ai2/asta-test/run_ann_learning_rule/run3/'
os.makedirs(WORK_DIR, exist_ok=True)
print(f'Working directory: {WORK_DIR}')

# ============================================================
# TASK CONFIGURATION
# ============================================================
N_BITS = 8
DELAY = 10
INPUT_DIM = N_BITS + 1  # +1 for recall cue marker
OUTPUT_DIM = N_BITS
HIDDEN_SIZE = 128
SEQ_LEN = N_BITS + DELAY + N_BITS  # = 26

print(f"\\nTask Configuration:")
print(f"  N_BITS={N_BITS}, DELAY={DELAY}, SEQ_LEN={SEQ_LEN}")
print(f"  INPUT_DIM={INPUT_DIM}, OUTPUT_DIM={OUTPUT_DIM}, HIDDEN_SIZE={HIDDEN_SIZE}")

# ============================================================
# COPY TASK DATA GENERATOR
# ============================================================
def generate_copy_batch(batch_size, n_bits=N_BITS, delay=DELAY, device=device):
    """
    Generate a batch of copy task sequences.
    Sequence structure:
      t=0..n_bits-1:           present input bits (marker=0)
      t=n_bits..n_bits+delay-2: blank steps (all zeros)
      t=n_bits+delay-1:        recall cue (marker bit=1)
      t=n_bits+delay..seq_len-1: output phase (target = original bits)
    """
    seq_len = n_bits + delay + n_bits
    input_dim = n_bits + 1
    
    patterns = torch.randint(0, 2, (batch_size, n_bits), dtype=torch.float32)
    
    inputs = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, input_dim)
    targets = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, n_bits)
    output_mask = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len)
    
    # Present input bits
    inputs[:, :n_bits, :n_bits] = patterns
    
    # Recall cue marker
    inputs[:, n_bits + delay - 1, n_bits] = 1.0
    
    # Output phase targets
    output_start = n_bits + delay
    targets[:, output_start:, :] = patterns.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, n_bits, -1)
    output_mask[:, output_start:] = 1.0
    
    return inputs.to(device), targets.to(device), output_mask.to(device)

# Test the data generator
batch_size = 4
torch.manual_seed(SEED)
inputs, targets, output_mask = generate_copy_batch(batch_size)

print(f"\\nBatch shapes:")
print(f"  inputs: {inputs.shape}")
print(f"  targets: {targets.shape}")
print(f"  output_mask: {output_mask.shape}")

print(f"\\nSample sequence (batch item 0):")
print(f"  Input bits (t=0..7):    {inputs[0, :N_BITS, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Input marker (t=17):    {inputs[0, N_BITS+DELAY-1, N_BITS].item()}")
print(f"  Target bits (t=18..25): {targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY:, :].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Output mask:            {output_mask[0].cpu().numpy()}")

# Verify pattern consistency
for i in range(batch_size):
    pat_in = inputs[i, :N_BITS, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()
    pat_out = targets[i, N_BITS+DELAY:, :].cpu().numpy()
    assert np.allclose(pat_in, pat_out), f"Mismatch at batch item {i}!"
print(f"\\n[PASS] Input/target consistency check passed for all {batch_size} items")

# Test multiple batches
for trial in range(3):
    inp, tgt, msk = generate_copy_batch(32)
    assert inp.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN, INPUT_DIM)
    assert tgt.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN, OUTPUT_DIM)
    assert msk.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN)
    assert msk[:, :N_BITS+DELAY].sum() == 0  # no targets during input+delay
    assert msk[:, N_BITS+DELAY:].sum() == 32 * N_BITS  # all output steps have targets
print(f"[PASS] Batch generation tests passed")

print(f"\\n=== Step 1: Environment Setup COMPLETE ===")
print(f"  PyTorch: {torch.__version__}")
print(f"  CUDA available: {torch.cuda.is_available()}")
print(f"  Device: {device}")
'''

    f.write(setup_code)

print('STDOUT:')
print(result.stdout)

    print('STDERR:', result.stderr[:1000])
print('Return code:', result.returncode)

An error occurred doing step 1. Let's try and debug the problem and retry (retry number 1).

Top-Level Task: Implement and test the PSC (Predictive Self-Correction) learning rule for RNNs on the copy task.

ALGORITHM:
PSC avoids backward propagation entirely. Each neuron has:
1. Main dynamics: h_t = tanh(W_rec * h_{t-1} + W_in * x_t + b)
2. Prediction compartment: p_i(t) = (1-beta)*p_i(t-1) + beta*(W_pred[i,:] @ h(t-1))
   This predicts the neuron's own activity from lateral connections.
3. Prediction error: delta_pred_i(t) = h_i(t) - p_i(t)
4. Eligibility trace: e_ij(t) = lambda*e_ij(t-1) + h_j(t-1)*dtanh_i(t)
5. Output error (only at output timesteps): err_i(t) = (y - target) projected back via W_out^T to hidden
6. Credit signal: s_i(t) = gamma*delta_pred_i(t) + (1-gamma)*output_err_i(t)
   (output_err_i is 0 except during output timesteps)
7. Oscillatory gate: gate(t) = max(0, sin(2*pi*t/T_theta))
8. Weight update: Delta_W_rec_ij = -lr * s_i(t) * e_ij(t) * gate(t)
9. Prediction weight update: Delta_W_pred_ij = -lr_pred * delta_pred_i(t) * h_j(t-1)

Use Adam momentum for weight updates.

EXPERIMENT:
- Task: Copy task — input 8 binary values, wait 10 blank steps, reproduce 8 values
- Architecture: Vanilla RNN, hidden size 128  
- Compare 4 methods:
  (a) BPTT (gold standard)
  (b) PSC with oscillatory gating (our method)
  (c) PSC WITHOUT oscillatory gating (ablation: always consolidate, gate=1 always)
  (d) FA (feedback alignment baseline, from previous experiments)
- Training: 5000 iterations, batch size 32
- PSC hyperparameter grid search (pick best per method):
  - lr: {0.001, 0.005, 0.01}
  - beta (prediction decay): {0.1, 0.3}
  - gamma (prediction vs output weight): {0.3, 0.5, 0.7}  
  - lambda (eligibility decay): {0.9, 0.95}
  - T_theta (oscillation period in timesteps): {10, 20}
  - lr_pred (prediction learning rate): 0.01
- For the grid search: do a quick 1000-iter run for each combo, pick top 3 configs, then run full 5000 iters
- Metrics: Final MSE, bit accuracy, convergence speed

IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION NOTES:
- W_pred is a SEPARATE weight matrix (128x128) for prediction, initialized randomly
- W_pred is learned via simple Hebbian prediction error rule (self-supervised)
- The main W_rec update uses the CREDIT SIGNAL (s_i) not the raw prediction error
- Output weights W_out are updated using standard (output_error * h_t) regardless of method
- Use grad clipping (norm 1.0) for stability

ENVIRONMENT: Use conda environment panda. PyTorch.
Save results as JSON.
Current Plan:
   1. Set up the environment: import necessary libraries (PyTorch, numpy, json, etc.), set random seeds for reproducibility, and define the copy task data generator (8 bits, 10-step delay).
   2. Implement the BPTT baseline: standard RNN with backpropagation through time, trained with Adam optimizer on the copy task.
   3. Implement the FA (Feedback Alignment) baseline: RNN where backward pass uses fixed random feedback matrices instead of transposed forward weights.
   4. Implement the PSC learning rule: RNN with prediction compartment (W_pred), eligibility traces, oscillatory gating, credit signal combining prediction error and output error, and Adam momentum for weight updates.
   5. Run the hyperparameter grid search for PSC (both with and without oscillatory gating): 1000-iteration quick runs for all combinations of lr, beta, gamma, lambda, T_theta. Record final MSE for each configuration.
   6. Select the top 3 hyperparameter configurations for PSC-with-gating and PSC-without-gating based on the grid search results.

Coding thoughts:
Thought: The bug is in how I'm building the input tensor. The copy task presents all 8 bits at each of the first 8 timesteps (all bits visible simultaneously at each input timestep), OR alternatively each bit is presented one per timestep. Looking at standard copy task implementations, at each input timestep t (0..7), the ENTIRE 8-bit pattern is visible at the input (i.e., all 8 bits are presented simultaneously). So inputs[:, :n_bits, :n_bits] should have shape (batch, 8, 8) where each of the 8 timesteps shows all 8 bits. This means I need to expand patterns: `inputs[:, :n_bits, :n_bits] = patterns.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, n_bits, -1)`. This way each of the 8 input timesteps shows all 8 bits. Actually that doesn't make sense either - why would you show all bits for all 8 timesteps? The standard copy task shows all bits simultaneously at t=0 only, OR shows one bit per timestep. Let me use the simplest approach: present all 8 bits at t=0 only (inputs[:, 0, :n_bits] = patterns), with subsequent input timesteps being blank until the recall cue. This is the cleanest version.
Action (code):
import subprocess

PYTHON = '/home/zihan.zhang/.conda/envs/panda/bin/python'
WORK_DIR = '/allen/programs/mindscope/workgroups/auto-model/zihan.zhang/ai2/asta-test/run_ann_learning_rule/run3/'

setup_code = '''
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import numpy as np
import json
import math
import os
import time
from itertools import product

# Set random seeds for reproducibility
SEED = 42
torch.manual_seed(SEED)
np.random.seed(SEED)

# Device
device = torch.device(\'cuda\' if torch.cuda.is_available() else \'cpu\')
print(f\'Using device: {device}\')
if torch.cuda.is_available():
    print(f\'GPU: {torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)}\')

# Working directory
WORK_DIR = \'/allen/programs/mindscope/workgroups/auto-model/zihan.zhang/ai2/asta-test/run_ann_learning_rule/run3/\'
os.makedirs(WORK_DIR, exist_ok=True)
print(f\'Working directory: {WORK_DIR}\')

# ============================================================
# TASK CONFIGURATION
# ============================================================
N_BITS = 8
DELAY = 10
# Copy task: present all 8 bits at t=0, blank for DELAY steps, recall cue at t=N_BITS+DELAY-1,
# then reproduce 8 bits at t=N_BITS+DELAY..N_BITS+DELAY+N_BITS-1
# Total sequence length = 1 (input) + (DELAY-1) (blank) + 1 (cue) + N_BITS (output) = N_BITS + DELAY + 1
# Actually let\'s use: N_BITS input timesteps (one bit per step) + DELAY blank + N_BITS output
# Each input timestep t presents bit t of the pattern
# This is the standard sequential copy task

INPUT_DIM = N_BITS + 1  # N_BITS channels + 1 recall cue channel
OUTPUT_DIM = N_BITS
HIDDEN_SIZE = 128
SEQ_LEN = N_BITS + DELAY + N_BITS  # 8 + 10 + 8 = 26

print(f"\nTask Configuration:")
print(f"  N_BITS={N_BITS}, DELAY={DELAY}, SEQ_LEN={SEQ_LEN}")
print(f"  INPUT_DIM={INPUT_DIM}, OUTPUT_DIM={OUTPUT_DIM}, HIDDEN_SIZE={HIDDEN_SIZE}")
print(f"  Sequence: [8 input steps] + [10 blank/delay steps] + [8 output steps]")
print(f"  At each input step t: present bit t of pattern in channel t")
print(f"  At t=17 (last delay step): recall cue marker=1")
print(f"  At t=18..25: network must reproduce the pattern")

# ============================================================
# COPY TASK DATA GENERATOR
# ============================================================
def generate_copy_batch(batch_size, n_bits=N_BITS, delay=DELAY, device=device):
    """
    Sequential copy task:
    - t=0..n_bits-1: input phase, at step t, bit t is presented in channel t
      (so at t=0: channel 0 = bit0, others 0; at t=1: channel 1 = bit1, etc.)
    - t=n_bits..n_bits+delay-2: blank (all zeros)
    - t=n_bits+delay-1: recall cue (marker channel = 1)
    - t=n_bits+delay..seq_len-1: output phase (target = original bits)
    
    Returns:
        inputs:      (batch_size, seq_len, input_dim)
        targets:     (batch_size, seq_len, n_bits)  -- nonzero only at output steps
        output_mask: (batch_size, seq_len)           -- 1 at output steps
    """
    seq_len = n_bits + delay + n_bits
    input_dim = n_bits + 1  # n_bits channels + 1 marker
    
    # Random binary patterns
    patterns = torch.randint(0, 2, (batch_size, n_bits), dtype=torch.float32)
    
    inputs = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, input_dim)
    targets = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, n_bits)
    output_mask = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len)
    
    # Input phase: at timestep t, present bit t in channel t
    for t in range(n_bits):
        inputs[:, t, t] = patterns[:, t]  # shape: (batch_size,)
    
    # Recall cue at t = n_bits + delay - 1
    inputs[:, n_bits + delay - 1, n_bits] = 1.0
    
    # Output phase
    output_start = n_bits + delay
    for t in range(n_bits):
        targets[:, output_start + t, :] = patterns  # all bits shown simultaneously in output
    output_mask[:, output_start:output_start + n_bits] = 1.0
    
    return inputs.to(device), targets.to(device), output_mask.to(device)

# Test the data generator
batch_size = 4
torch.manual_seed(SEED)
inputs, targets, output_mask = generate_copy_batch(batch_size)

print(f"\nBatch shapes:")
print(f"  inputs: {inputs.shape}")
print(f"  targets: {targets.shape}")
print(f"  output_mask: {output_mask.shape}")

print(f"\nSample sequence (batch item 0):")
print(f"  Pattern to copy: {targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY, :].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Input at t=0 (ch0-7): {inputs[0, 0, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Input at t=1 (ch0-7): {inputs[0, 1, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Input at t=7 (ch0-7): {inputs[0, 7, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Recall marker at t=17: {inputs[0, N_BITS+DELAY-1, N_BITS].item()}")
print(f"  Target at t=18: {targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY, :].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Target at t=25: {targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY+N_BITS-1, :].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Output mask: {output_mask[0].cpu().numpy()}")

# Verify correctness
for i in range(batch_size):
    # Extract original pattern from input
    pat_from_input = torch.zeros(N_BITS)
    for t in range(N_BITS):
        pat_from_input[t] = inputs[i, t, t].cpu()
    # Check targets match
    for t in range(N_BITS):
        tgt_t = targets[i, N_BITS+DELAY+t, :].cpu()
        assert torch.allclose(tgt_t, pat_from_input), f"Mismatch at batch {i}, output step {t}!"
print(f"\n[PASS] Input/target consistency check passed for all {batch_size} items")

# Test batch dimensions
for trial in range(3):
    inp, tgt, msk = generate_copy_batch(32)
    assert inp.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN, INPUT_DIM), f"Wrong input shape: {inp.shape}"
    assert tgt.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN, OUTPUT_DIM), f"Wrong target shape: {tgt.shape}"
    assert msk.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN), f"Wrong mask shape: {msk.shape}"
    # Only output phase has nonzero mask
    assert msk[:, :N_BITS+DELAY].sum().item() == 0, "Non-zero mask in input/delay phase!"
    expected_ones = 32 * N_BITS
    actual_ones = msk[:, N_BITS+DELAY:].sum().item()
    assert actual_ones == expected_ones, f"Expected {expected_ones} mask=1, got {actual_ones}"
print(f"[PASS] All batch shape/mask tests passed")

print(f"\n=== Step 1: Environment Setup COMPLETE ===")
print(f"  PyTorch: {torch.__version__}")
print(f"  CUDA: {torch.cuda.is_available()}, Device: {device}")

In [22]: setup_code = '''
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import numpy as np
import json
import math
import os
import time
from itertools import product

# Set random seeds for reproducibility
SEED = 42
torch.manual_seed(SEED)
np.random.seed(SEED)

# Device
device = torch.device(\'cuda\' if torch.cuda.is_available() else \'cpu\')
print(f\'Using device: {device}\')
if torch.cuda.is_available():
    print(f\'GPU: {torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)}\')

# Working directory
WORK_DIR = \'/allen/programs/mindscope/workgroups/auto-model/zihan.zhang/ai2/asta-test/run_ann_learning_rule/run3/\'
os.makedirs(WORK_DIR, exist_ok=True)
print(f\'Working directory: {WORK_DIR}\')

# ============================================================
# TASK CONFIGURATION
# ============================================================
N_BITS = 8
DELAY = 10
# Copy task: present all 8 bits at t=0, blank for DELAY steps, recall cue at t=N_BITS+DELAY-1,
# then reproduce 8 bits at t=N_BITS+DELAY..N_BITS+DELAY+N_BITS-1
# Total sequence length = 1 (input) + (DELAY-1) (blank) + 1 (cue) + N_BITS (output) = N_BITS + DELAY + 1
# Actually let\'s use: N_BITS input timesteps (one bit per step) + DELAY blank + N_BITS output
# Each input timestep t presents bit t of the pattern
# This is the standard sequential copy task

INPUT_DIM = N_BITS + 1  # N_BITS channels + 1 recall cue channel
OUTPUT_DIM = N_BITS
HIDDEN_SIZE = 128
SEQ_LEN = N_BITS + DELAY + N_BITS  # 8 + 10 + 8 = 26

print(f"\nTask Configuration:")
print(f"  N_BITS={N_BITS}, DELAY={DELAY}, SEQ_LEN={SEQ_LEN}")
print(f"  INPUT_DIM={INPUT_DIM}, OUTPUT_DIM={OUTPUT_DIM}, HIDDEN_SIZE={HIDDEN_SIZE}")
print(f"  Sequence: [8 input steps] + [10 blank/delay steps] + [8 output steps]")
print(f"  At each input step t: present bit t of pattern in channel t")
print(f"  At t=17 (last delay step): recall cue marker=1")
print(f"  At t=18..25: network must reproduce the pattern")

# ============================================================
# COPY TASK DATA GENERATOR
# ============================================================
def generate_copy_batch(batch_size, n_bits=N_BITS, delay=DELAY, device=device):
    """
    Sequential copy task:
    - t=0..n_bits-1: input phase, at step t, bit t is presented in channel t
      (so at t=0: channel 0 = bit0, others 0; at t=1: channel 1 = bit1, etc.)
    - t=n_bits..n_bits+delay-2: blank (all zeros)
    - t=n_bits+delay-1: recall cue (marker channel = 1)
    - t=n_bits+delay..seq_len-1: output phase (target = original bits)
    
    Returns:
        inputs:      (batch_size, seq_len, input_dim)
        targets:     (batch_size, seq_len, n_bits)  -- nonzero only at output steps
        output_mask: (batch_size, seq_len)           -- 1 at output steps
    """
    seq_len = n_bits + delay + n_bits
    input_dim = n_bits + 1  # n_bits channels + 1 marker
    
    # Random binary patterns
    patterns = torch.randint(0, 2, (batch_size, n_bits), dtype=torch.float32)
    
    inputs = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, input_dim)
    targets = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, n_bits)
    output_mask = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len)
    
    # Input phase: at timestep t, present bit t in channel t
    for t in range(n_bits):
        inputs[:, t, t] = patterns[:, t]  # shape: (batch_size,)
    
    # Recall cue at t = n_bits + delay - 1
    inputs[:, n_bits + delay - 1, n_bits] = 1.0
    
    # Output phase
    output_start = n_bits + delay
    for t in range(n_bits):
        targets[:, output_start + t, :] = patterns  # all bits shown simultaneously in output
    output_mask[:, output_start:output_start + n_bits] = 1.0
    
    return inputs.to(device), targets.to(device), output_mask.to(device)

# Test the data generator
batch_size = 4
torch.manual_seed(SEED)
inputs, targets, output_mask = generate_copy_batch(batch_size)

print(f"\nBatch shapes:")
print(f"  inputs: {inputs.shape}")
print(f"  targets: {targets.shape}")
print(f"  output_mask: {output_mask.shape}")

print(f"\nSample sequence (batch item 0):")
print(f"  Pattern to copy: {targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY, :].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Input at t=0 (ch0-7): {inputs[0, 0, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Input at t=1 (ch0-7): {inputs[0, 1, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Input at t=7 (ch0-7): {inputs[0, 7, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Recall marker at t=17: {inputs[0, N_BITS+DELAY-1, N_BITS].item()}")
print(f"  Target at t=18: {targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY, :].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Target at t=25: {targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY+N_BITS-1, :].cpu().numpy()}")
print(f"  Output mask: {output_mask[0].cpu().numpy()}")

# Verify correctness
for i in range(batch_size):
    # Extract original pattern from input
    pat_from_input = torch.zeros(N_BITS)
    for t in range(N_BITS):
        pat_from_input[t] = inputs[i, t, t].cpu()
    # Check targets match
    for t in range(N_BITS):
        tgt_t = targets[i, N_BITS+DELAY+t, :].cpu()
        assert torch.allclose(tgt_t, pat_from_input), f"Mismatch at batch {i}, output step {t}!"
print(f"\n[PASS] Input/target consistency check passed for all {batch_size} items")

# Test batch dimensions
for trial in range(3):
    inp, tgt, msk = generate_copy_batch(32)
    assert inp.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN, INPUT_DIM), f"Wrong input shape: {inp.shape}"
    assert tgt.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN, OUTPUT_DIM), f"Wrong target shape: {tgt.shape}"
    assert msk.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN), f"Wrong mask shape: {msk.shape}"
    # Only output phase has nonzero mask
    assert msk[:, :N_BITS+DELAY].sum().item() == 0, "Non-zero mask in input/delay phase!"
    expected_ones = 32 * N_BITS
    actual_ones = msk[:, N_BITS+DELAY:].sum().item()
    assert actual_ones == expected_ones, f"Expected {expected_ones} mask=1, got {actual_ones}"
print(f"[PASS] All batch shape/mask tests passed")

print(f"\n=== Step 1: Environment Setup COMPLETE ===")
print(f"  PyTorch: {torch.__version__}")
print(f"  CUDA: {torch.cuda.is_available()}, Device: {device}")

An error occurred doing step 1. Let's try and debug the problem and retry (retry number 2).

Top-Level Task: Implement and test the PSC (Predictive Self-Correction) learning rule for RNNs on the copy task.

ALGORITHM:
PSC avoids backward propagation entirely. Each neuron has:
1. Main dynamics: h_t = tanh(W_rec * h_{t-1} + W_in * x_t + b)
2. Prediction compartment: p_i(t) = (1-beta)*p_i(t-1) + beta*(W_pred[i,:] @ h(t-1))
   This predicts the neuron's own activity from lateral connections.
3. Prediction error: delta_pred_i(t) = h_i(t) - p_i(t)
4. Eligibility trace: e_ij(t) = lambda*e_ij(t-1) + h_j(t-1)*dtanh_i(t)
5. Output error (only at output timesteps): err_i(t) = (y - target) projected back via W_out^T to hidden
6. Credit signal: s_i(t) = gamma*delta_pred_i(t) + (1-gamma)*output_err_i(t)
   (output_err_i is 0 except during output timesteps)
7. Oscillatory gate: gate(t) = max(0, sin(2*pi*t/T_theta))
8. Weight update: Delta_W_rec_ij = -lr * s_i(t) * e_ij(t) * gate(t)
9. Prediction weight update: Delta_W_pred_ij = -lr_pred * delta_pred_i(t) * h_j(t-1)

Use Adam momentum for weight updates.

EXPERIMENT:
- Task: Copy task — input 8 binary values, wait 10 blank steps, reproduce 8 values
- Architecture: Vanilla RNN, hidden size 128  
- Compare 4 methods:
  (a) BPTT (gold standard)
  (b) PSC with oscillatory gating (our method)
  (c) PSC WITHOUT oscillatory gating (ablation: always consolidate, gate=1 always)
  (d) FA (feedback alignment baseline, from previous experiments)
- Training: 5000 iterations, batch size 32
- PSC hyperparameter grid search (pick best per method):
  - lr: {0.001, 0.005, 0.01}
  - beta (prediction decay): {0.1, 0.3}
  - gamma (prediction vs output weight): {0.3, 0.5, 0.7}  
  - lambda (eligibility decay): {0.9, 0.95}
  - T_theta (oscillation period in timesteps): {10, 20}
  - lr_pred (prediction learning rate): 0.01
- For the grid search: do a quick 1000-iter run for each combo, pick top 3 configs, then run full 5000 iters
- Metrics: Final MSE, bit accuracy, convergence speed

IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION NOTES:
- W_pred is a SEPARATE weight matrix (128x128) for prediction, initialized randomly
- W_pred is learned via simple Hebbian prediction error rule (self-supervised)
- The main W_rec update uses the CREDIT SIGNAL (s_i) not the raw prediction error
- Output weights W_out are updated using standard (output_error * h_t) regardless of method
- Use grad clipping (norm 1.0) for stability

ENVIRONMENT: Use conda environment panda. PyTorch.
Save results as JSON.
Current Plan:
   1. Set up the environment: import necessary libraries (PyTorch, numpy, json, etc.), set random seeds for reproducibility, and define the copy task data generator (8 bits, 10-step delay).
   2. Implement the BPTT baseline: standard RNN with backpropagation through time, trained with Adam optimizer on the copy task.
   3. Implement the FA (Feedback Alignment) baseline: RNN where backward pass uses fixed random feedback matrices instead of transposed forward weights.
   4. Implement the PSC learning rule: RNN with prediction compartment (W_pred), eligibility traces, oscillatory gating, credit signal combining prediction error and output error, and Adam momentum for weight updates.
   5. Run the hyperparameter grid search for PSC (both with and without oscillatory gating): 1000-iteration quick runs for all combinations of lr, beta, gamma, lambda, T_theta. Record final MSE for each configuration.
   6. Select the top 3 hyperparameter configurations for PSC-with-gating and PSC-without-gating based on the grid search results.
   7. Run full 5000-iteration training for: (a) BPTT, (b) best PSC with oscillatory gating, (c) best PSC without oscillatory gating, (d) FA baseline. Record MSE and bit accuracy at regular intervals.
   8. Analyze and visualize results: plot learning curves, compare final MSE and bit accuracy across methods, compute convergence speed metrics.
   9. Save all results to JSON and write a comprehensive report summarizing findings.

script_path = os.path.join(WORK_DIR, 'step1_setup.py')

lines = [
    'import torch',
    'import torch.nn as nn',
    'import numpy as np',
    'import json',
    'import math',
    'import os',
    'import time',
    'from itertools import product',
    '',
    'SEED = 42',
    'torch.manual_seed(SEED)',
    'np.random.seed(SEED)',
    '',
    'device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")',
    'print("Using device:", device)',
    'if torch.cuda.is_available():',
    '    print("GPU:", torch.cuda.get_device_name(0))',
    '',
    'WORK_DIR = "/allen/programs/mindscope/workgroups/auto-model/zihan.zhang/ai2/asta-test/run_ann_learning_rule/run3/"',
    'os.makedirs(WORK_DIR, exist_ok=True)',
    'print("Working directory:", WORK_DIR)',
    '',
    '# Task configuration',
    'N_BITS = 8',
    'DELAY = 10',
    'INPUT_DIM = N_BITS + 1',
    'OUTPUT_DIM = N_BITS',
    'HIDDEN_SIZE = 128',
    'SEQ_LEN = N_BITS + DELAY + N_BITS',
    '',
    'print("")',
    'print("Task Configuration:")',
    'print("  N_BITS=", N_BITS, " DELAY=", DELAY, " SEQ_LEN=", SEQ_LEN)',
    'print("  INPUT_DIM=", INPUT_DIM, " OUTPUT_DIM=", OUTPUT_DIM, " HIDDEN_SIZE=", HIDDEN_SIZE)',
    '',
    'def generate_copy_batch(batch_size, n_bits=N_BITS, delay=DELAY, dev=None):',
    '    if dev is None:',
    '        dev = device',
    '    seq_len = n_bits + delay + n_bits',
    '    input_dim = n_bits + 1',
    '    patterns = torch.randint(0, 2, (batch_size, n_bits), dtype=torch.float32)',
    '    inputs = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, input_dim)',
    '    targets = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, n_bits)',
    '    output_mask = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len)',
    '    for t in range(n_bits):',
    '        inputs[:, t, t] = patterns[:, t]',
    '    inputs[:, n_bits + delay - 1, n_bits] = 1.0',
    '    output_start = n_bits + delay',
    '    for t in range(n_bits):',
    '        targets[:, output_start + t, :] = patterns',
    '    output_mask[:, output_start:output_start + n_bits] = 1.0',
    '    return inputs.to(dev), targets.to(dev), output_mask.to(dev)',
    '',
    '# Test the data generator',
    'batch_size = 4',
    'torch.manual_seed(SEED)',
    'inputs, targets, output_mask = generate_copy_batch(batch_size)',
    '',
    'print("")',
    'print("Batch shapes:")',
    'print("  inputs:", inputs.shape)',
    'print("  targets:", targets.shape)',
    'print("  output_mask:", output_mask.shape)',
    '',
    'print("")',
    'print("Sample sequence (batch item 0):")',
    'print("  Pattern to copy:", targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY, :].cpu().numpy())',
    'print("  Input at t=0:", inputs[0, 0, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy())',
    'print("  Input at t=3:", inputs[0, 3, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy())',
    'print("  Input at t=7:", inputs[0, 7, :N_BITS].cpu().numpy())',
    'print("  Recall cue at t=17:", inputs[0, N_BITS+DELAY-1, N_BITS].item())',
    'print("  Target at t=18:", targets[0, N_BITS+DELAY, :].cpu().numpy())',
    'print("  Output mask:", output_mask[0].cpu().numpy())',
    '',
    '# Verify correctness',
    'for i in range(batch_size):',
    '    pat_from_input = torch.zeros(N_BITS)',
    '    for t in range(N_BITS):',
    '        pat_from_input[t] = inputs[i, t, t].cpu()',
    '    for t in range(N_BITS):',
    '        tgt_t = targets[i, N_BITS+DELAY+t, :].cpu()',
    '        assert torch.allclose(tgt_t, pat_from_input), "Mismatch at batch " + str(i)',
    'print("[PASS] Input/target consistency check passed")',
    '',
    '# Test batch dimensions',
    'for trial in range(3):',
    '    inp, tgt, msk = generate_copy_batch(32)',
    '    assert inp.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN, INPUT_DIM)',
    '    assert tgt.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN, OUTPUT_DIM)',
    '    assert msk.shape == (32, SEQ_LEN)',
    '    assert msk[:, :N_BITS+DELAY].sum().item() == 0',
    '    assert msk[:, N_BITS+DELAY:].sum().item() == 32 * N_BITS',
    'print("[PASS] Batch shape/mask tests passed")',
    '',
    'print("")',
    'print("=== Step 1: Environment Setup COMPLETE ===")',
    'print("  PyTorch:", torch.__version__)',
    'print("  CUDA:", torch.cuda.is_available(), "Device:", device)',
]

with open(script_path, 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))

print(f'Script written to {script_path}')

----------------------------------------

Step 1 complete. Moving onto the next step in the plan...

Top-Level Task: Implement and test the PSC (Predictive Self-Correction) learning rule for RNNs on the copy task.

ALGORITHM:
PSC avoids backward propagation entirely. Each neuron has:
1. Main dynamics: h_t = tanh(W_rec * h_{t-1} + W_in * x_t + b)
2. Prediction compartment: p_i(t) = (1-beta)*p_i(t-1) + beta*(W_pred[i,:] @ h(t-1))
   This predicts the neuron's own activity from lateral connections.
3. Prediction error: delta_pred_i(t) = h_i(t) - p_i(t)
4. Eligibility trace: e_ij(t) = lambda*e_ij(t-1) + h_j(t-1)*dtanh_i(t)
5. Output error (only at output timesteps): err_i(t) = (y - target) projected back via W_out^T to hidden
6. Credit signal: s_i(t) = gamma*delta_pred_i(t) + (1-gamma)*output_err_i(t)
   (output_err_i is 0 except during output timesteps)
7. Oscillatory gate: gate(t) = max(0, sin(2*pi*t/T_theta))
8. Weight update: Delta_W_rec_ij = -lr * s_i(t) * e_ij(t) * gate(t)
9. Prediction weight update: Delta_W_pred_ij = -lr_pred * delta_pred_i(t) * h_j(t-1)

Use Adam momentum for weight updates.

EXPERIMENT:
- Task: Copy task — input 8 binary values, wait 10 blank steps, reproduce 8 values
- Architecture: Vanilla RNN, hidden size 128  
- Compare 4 methods:
  (a) BPTT (gold standard)
  (b) PSC with oscillatory gating (our method)
  (c) PSC WITHOUT oscillatory gating (ablation: always consolidate, gate=1 always)
  (d) FA (feedback alignment baseline, from previous experiments)
- Training: 5000 iterations, batch size 32
- PSC hyperparameter grid search (pick best per method):
  - lr: {0.001, 0.005, 0.01}
  - beta (prediction decay): {0.1, 0.3}
  - gamma (prediction vs output weight): {0.3, 0.5, 0.7}  
  - lambda (eligibility decay): {0.9, 0.95}
  - T_theta (oscillation period in timesteps): {10, 20}
  - lr_pred (prediction learning rate): 0.01
- For the grid search: do a quick 1000-iter run for each combo, pick top 3 configs, then run full 5000 iters
- Metrics: Final MSE, bit accuracy, convergence speed

IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION NOTES:
- W_pred is a SEPARATE weight matrix (128x128) for prediction, initialized randomly
- W_pred is learned via simple Hebbian prediction error rule (self-supervised)
- The main W_rec update uses the CREDIT SIGNAL (s_i) not the raw prediction error
- Output weights W_out are updated using standard (output_error * h_t) regardless of method
- Use grad clipping (norm 1.0) for stability

ENVIRONMENT: Use conda environment panda. PyTorch.
Save results as JSON.
Current Plan:
   1. Set up the environment: import necessary libraries (PyTorch, numpy, json, etc.), set random seeds for reproducibility, and define the copy task data generator (8 bits, 10-step delay).
   2. Implement the BPTT baseline: standard RNN with backpropagation through time, trained with Adam optimizer on the copy task.
   3. Implement the FA (Feedback Alignment) baseline: RNN where backward pass uses fixed random feedback matrices instead of transposed forward weights.
   4. Implement the PSC learning rule: RNN with prediction compartment (W_pred), eligibility traces, oscillatory gating, credit signal combining prediction error and output error, and Adam momentum for weight updates.
   5. Run the hyperparameter grid search for PSC (both with and without oscillatory gating): 1000-iteration quick runs for all combinations of lr, beta, gamma, lambda, T_theta. Record final MSE for each configuration.
   6. Select the top 3 hyperparameter configurations for PSC-with-gating and PSC-without-gating based on the grid search results.
   7. Run full 5000-iteration training for: (a) BPTT, (b) best PSC with oscillatory gating, (c) best PSC without oscillatory gating, (d) FA baseline. Record MSE and bit accuracy at regular intervals.
   8. Analyze and visualize results: plot learning curves, compare final MSE and bit accuracy across methods, compute convergence speed metrics.
   9. Save all results to JSON and write a comprehensive report summarizing findings.

============================== Panda ==============================
Coding thoughts:
Thought: I need to implement the BPTT baseline RNN and run a quick test to verify it works. I'll write this as a Python script file and execute it with the panda Python. The BPTT RNN should: (1) have W_rec, W_in, W_out, biases as parameters, (2) use standard tanh activation, (3) be trained with Adam optimizer, (4) use MSE loss only at output timesteps. I'll run a quick 200-iteration test to verify it's learning, then save the implementation to a shared module file that will be reused in subsequent steps.
Action (code):
import subprocess
import os

PYTHON = '/home/zihan.zhang/.conda/envs/panda/bin/python'
WORK_DIR = '/allen/programs/mindscope/workgroups/auto-model/zihan.zhang/ai2/asta-test/run_ann_learning_rule/run3/'

script_path = os.path.join(WORK_DIR, 'step2_bptt.py')

lines = [
    'import torch',
    'import torch.nn as nn',
    'import numpy as np',
    'import json',
    'import os',
    'import math',
    '',
    'SEED = 42',
    'torch.manual_seed(SEED)',
    'np.random.seed(SEED)',
    '',
    'device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")',
    'print("Device:", device)',
    '',
    'WORK_DIR = "/allen/programs/mindscope/workgroups/auto-model/zihan.zhang/ai2/asta-test/run_ann_learning_rule/run3/"',
    'N_BITS = 8',
    'DELAY = 10',
    'INPUT_DIM = N_BITS + 1',
    'OUTPUT_DIM = N_BITS',
    'HIDDEN_SIZE = 128',
    'SEQ_LEN = N_BITS + DELAY + N_BITS',
    '',
    '# ============================================================',
    '# COPY TASK DATA GENERATOR',
    '# ============================================================',
    'def generate_copy_batch(batch_size, n_bits=N_BITS, delay=DELAY, dev=None):',
    '    if dev is None:',
    '        dev = device',
    '    seq_len = n_bits + delay + n_bits',
    '    input_dim = n_bits + 1',
    '    patterns = torch.randint(0, 2, (batch_size, n_bits), dtype=torch.float32)',
    '    inputs = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, input_dim)',
    '    targets = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len, n_bits)',
    '    output_mask = torch.zeros(batch_size, seq_len)',
    '    for t in range(n_bits):',
    '        inputs[:, t, t] = patterns[:, t]',
    '    inputs[:, n_bits + delay - 1, n_bits] = 1.0',
    '    output_start = n_bits + delay',
    '    for t in range(n_bits):',
    '        targets[:, output_start + t, :] = patterns',
    '    output_mask[:, output_start:output_start + n_bits] = 1.0',
    '    return inputs.to(dev), targets.to(dev), output_mask.to(dev)',
    '',
    '# ============================================================',
    '# BPTT RNN MODEL',
    '# ============================================================',
    'class VanillaRNN(nn.Module):',
    '    def __init__(self, input_dim, hidden_size, output_dim):',
    '        super(VanillaRNN, self).__init__()',
    '        self.hidden_size = hidden_size',
    '        self.W_in = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(hidden_size, input_dim) * 0.1)',
    '        self.W_rec = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(hidden_size, hidden_size) * 0.1)',
    '        self.b_rec = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(hidden_size))',
    '        self.W_out = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(output_dim, hidden_size) * 0.1)',
    '        self.b_out = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(output_dim))',
    '',
    '    def forward(self, inputs, h0=None):',
    '        batch_size, seq_len, _ = inputs.shape',
    '        if h0 is None:',
    '            h = torch.zeros(batch_size, self.hidden_size, device=inputs.device)',
    '        else:',
    '            h = h0',
    '        outputs = []',
    '        for t in range(seq_len):',
    '            x_t = inputs[:, t, :]',
    '            a_t = h @ self.W_rec.T + x_t @ self.W_in.T + self.b_rec',
    '            h = torch.tanh(a_t)',
    '            y_t = h @ self.W_out.T + self.b_out',
    '            outputs.append(y_t)',
    '        outputs = torch.stack(outputs, dim=1)',
    '        return outputs, h',
    '',
    'def compute_metrics(outputs, targets, output_mask):',
    '    mask = output_mask.unsqueeze(-1)',
    '    pred = outputs * mask',
    '    tgt = targets * mask',
    '    mse = ((pred - tgt) ** 2).sum() / (mask.sum() * targets.shape[-1])',
    '    pred_bits = (outputs > 0.5).float() * output_mask.unsqueeze(-1)',
    '    tgt_bits = targets * output_mask.unsqueeze(-1)',
    '    correct = (pred_bits == tgt_bits).float() * output_mask.unsqueeze(-1)',
    '    n_total = output_mask.sum() * targets.shape[-1]',
    '    acc = correct.sum() / n_total if n_total > 0 else torch.tensor(0.0)',
    '    return mse.item(), acc.item()',
    '',
    '# ============================================================',
    '# BPTT TRAINING FUNCTION',
    '# ============================================================',
    'def train_bptt(n_iter=200, batch_size=32, lr=0.001, verbose=True, log_interval=50):',
    '    torch.manual_seed(SEED)',
    '    model = VanillaRNN(INPUT_DIM, HIDDEN_SIZE, OUTPUT_DIM).to(device)',
    '    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=lr)',
    '    losses = []',
    '    mse_history = []',
    '    acc_history = []',
    '    for it in range(n_iter):',
    '        inputs, targets, output_mask = generate_copy_batch(batch_size)',
    '        optimizer.zero_grad()',
    '        outputs, _ = model(inputs)',
    '        mask = output_mask.unsqueeze(-1)',
    '        loss = ((outputs - targets) ** 2 * mask).sum() / (mask.sum() * OUTPUT_DIM)',
    '        loss.backward()',
    '        torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(model.parameters(), 1.0)',
    '        optimizer.step()',
    '        mse, acc = compute_metrics(outputs.detach(), targets, output_mask)',
    '        losses.append(loss.item())',
    '        mse_history.append(mse)',
    '        acc_history.append(acc)',
    '        if verbose and (it + 1) % log_interval == 0:',
    '            print("  Iter", it+1, "| Loss:", round(loss.item(), 5),',
    '                  "| MSE:", round(mse, 5), "| Acc:", round(acc, 4))',
    '    return model, losses, mse_history, acc_history',
    '',
    '# ============================================================',
    '# QUICK TEST: 200 iterations to verify BPTT works',
    '# ============================================================',
    'print("")',
    'print("=== Testing BPTT (200 iterations) ===")',
    'model, losses, mse_history, acc_history = train_bptt(n_iter=200, lr=0.001, log_interval=50)',
    'print("")',
    'print("BPTT Quick Test Results:")',
    'print("  Initial MSE:", round(mse_history[0], 5))',
    'print("  Final MSE:", round(mse_history[-1], 5))',
    'print("  Initial Acc:", round(acc_history[0], 4))',
    'print("  Final Acc:", round(acc_history[-1], 4))',
    'print("  Loss improved:", mse_history[-1] < mse_history[0])',
    '',
    '# Check model parameters exist',
    'print("")',
    'print("Model parameters:")',
    'for name, param in model.named_parameters():',
    '    print("  ", name, param.shape)',
    '',
    '# Run longer test to see convergence',
    'print("")',
    'print("=== Testing BPTT (500 iterations, lr=0.005) ===")',
    'model2, losses2, mse2, acc2 = train_bptt(n_iter=500, lr=0.005, log_interval=100)',
    'print("")',
    'print("BPTT 500-iter Results:")',
    'print("  Initial MSE:", round(mse2[0], 5))',
    'print("  Final MSE:", round(mse2[-1], 5))',
    'print("  Final Acc:", round(acc2[-1], 4))',
    '',
    'print("")',
    'print("=== Step 2: BPTT Implementation VERIFIED ===")',
]

with open(script_path, 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))

result = subprocess.run([PYTHON, script_path], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=120)
print('STDOUT:')
print(result.stdout)
if result.stderr:
    print('STDERR:', result.stderr[:2000])
print('Return code:', result.returncode)

----------------------------------------

Step 2 complete. Moving onto the next step in the plan...

Top-Level Task: Implement and test the PSC (Predictive Self-Correction) learning rule for RNNs on the copy task.

ALGORITHM:
PSC avoids backward propagation entirely. Each neuron has:
1. Main dynamics: h_t = tanh(W_rec * h_{t-1} + W_in * x_t + b)
2. Prediction compartment: p_i(t) = (1-beta)*p_i(t-1) + beta*(W_pred[i,:] @ h(t-1))
   This predicts the neuron's own activity from lateral connections.
3. Prediction error: delta_pred_i(t) = h_i(t) - p_i(t)
4. Eligibility trace: e_ij(t) = lambda*e_ij(t-1) + h_j(t-1)*dtanh_i(t)
5. Output error (only at output timesteps): err_i(t) = (y - target) projected back via W_out^T to hidden
6. Credit signal: s_i(t) = gamma*delta_pred_i(t) + (1-gamma)*output_err_i(t)
   (output_err_i is 0 except during output timesteps)
7. Oscillatory gate: gate(t) = max(0, sin(2*pi*t/T_theta))
8. Weight update: Delta_W_rec_ij = -lr * s_i(t) * e_ij(t) * gate(t)
9. Prediction weight update: Delta_W_pred_ij = -lr_pred * delta_pred_i(t) * h_j(t-1)

Use Adam momentum for weight updates.

EXPERIMENT:
- Task: Copy task — input 8 binary values, wait 10 blank steps, reproduce 8 values
- Architecture: Vanilla RNN, hidden size 128  
- Compare 4 methods:
  (a) BPTT (gold standard)
  (b) PSC with oscillatory gating (our method)
  (c) PSC WITHOUT oscillatory gating (ablation: always consolidate, gate=1 always)
  (d) FA (feedback alignment baseline, from previous experiments)
- Training: 5000 iterations, batch size 32
- PSC hyperparameter grid search (pick best per method):
  - lr: {0.001, 0.005, 0.01}
  - beta (prediction decay): {0.1, 0.3}
  - gamma (prediction vs output weight): {0.3, 0.5, 0.7}  
  - lambda (eligibility decay): {0.9, 0.95}
  - T_theta (oscillation period in timesteps): {10, 20}
  - lr_pred (prediction learning rate): 0.01
- For the grid search: do a quick 1000-iter run for each combo, pick top 3 configs, then run full 5000 iters
- Metrics: Final MSE, bit accuracy, convergence speed

IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION NOTES:
- W_pred is a SEPARATE weight matrix (128x128) for prediction, initialized randomly
- W_pred is learned via simple Hebbian prediction error rule (self-supervised)
- The main W_rec update uses the CREDIT SIGNAL (s_i) not the raw prediction error
- Output weights W_out are updated using standard (output_error * h_t) regardless of method
- Use grad clipping (norm 1.0) for stability

ENVIRONMENT: Use conda environment panda. PyTorch.
Save results as JSON.
Current Plan:
   1. Set up the environment: import necessary libraries (PyTorch, numpy, json, etc.), set random seeds for reproducibility, and define the copy task data generator (8 bits, 10-step delay).
   2. Implement the BPTT baseline: standard RNN with backpropagation through time, trained with Adam optimizer on the copy task.
   3. Implement the FA (Feedback Alignment) baseline: RNN where backward pass uses fixed random feedback matrices instead of transposed forward weights.
   4. Implement the PSC learning rule: RNN with prediction compartment (W_pred), eligibility traces, oscillatory gating, credit signal combining prediction error and output error, and Adam momentum for weight updates.
   5. Run the hyperparameter grid search for PSC (both with and without oscillatory gating): 1000-iteration quick runs for all combinations of lr, beta, gamma, lambda, T_theta. Record final MSE for each configuration.
   6. Select the top 3 hyperparameter configurations for PSC-with-gating and PSC-without-gating based on the grid search results.
   7. Run full 5000-iteration training for: (a) BPTT, (b) best PSC with oscillatory gating, (c) best PSC without oscillatory gating, (d) FA baseline. Record MSE and bit accuracy at regular intervals.
   8. Analyze and visualize results: plot learning curves, compare final MSE and bit accuracy across methods, compute convergence speed metrics.
   9. Save all results to JSON and write a comprehensive report summarizing findings.

targets*mask)**2).sum() / (mask.sum()*targets.shape[-1])',\n    '    pred_bits = (outputs > 0.5).float()',\n    '    correct = ((pred_bits == targets).float() * mask).sum()',\n    '    return mse.item(), (correct / (mask.sum()*targets.shape[-1])).item()',\n    '',\n    '# ============================================================',\n    '# FA TRAINING: manually implement forward + FA backward',\n    '# ============================================================',\n    '# FA uses fixed random feedback matrices B_rec (hidden->hidden) and B_out (out->hidden)',\n    '# instead of W_rec^T and W_out^T during backward pass',\n    '',\n    'def train_fa(n_iter=200, batch_size=32, lr=0.001, verbose=True, log_interval=50):',\n    '    torch.manual_seed(SEED)',\n    '    # Weight matrices',\n    '    W_in  = (torch.randn(HIDDEN_SIZE, INPUT_DIM) * 0.1).to(device).requires_grad_(False)',\n    '    W_rec = (torch.randn(HIDDEN_SIZE, HIDDEN_SIZE) * 0.1).to(device).requires_grad_(False)',\n    '    b_rec = torch.zeros(HIDDEN_SIZE).to(device).requires_grad_(False)',\n    '    W_out = (torch.randn(OUTPUT_DIM, HIDDEN_SIZE) * 0.1).to(device).requires_grad_(False)',\n    '    b_out = torch.zeros(OUTPUT_DIM).to(device).requires_grad_(False)',\n    '    # Fixed random feedback matrices (FA key component)',\n    '    B_rec = (torch.randn(HIDDEN_SIZE, HIDDEN_SIZE) * 0.1).to(device)',\n    '    B_out = (torch.randn(HIDDEN_SIZE, OUTPUT_DIM) * 0.1).to(device)',\n    '    # Adam state',\n    '    params = [W_in, W_rec, b_rec, W_out, b_out]',\n    '    m = [torch.zeros_like(p) for p in params]',\n    '    v = [torch.zeros_like(p) for p in params]',\n    '    beta1, beta2, eps = 0.9, 0.999, 1e-8',\n    '    mse_history, acc_history = [], []',\n    '    for it in range(n_iter):',\n    '        inputs, targets, output_mask = generate_copy_batch(batch_size)',\n    '        # Forward pass - store activations',\n    '        h = torch.zeros(batch_size, HIDDEN_SIZE, device=device)',\n    '        hs = []  # hidden states',\n    '        as_ = []  # pre-activation',\n    '        outputs = []',\n    '        for t in range(SEQ_LEN):',\n    '            x_t = inputs[:, t, :]',\n    '            a_t = h @ W_rec.T + x_t @ W_in.T + b_rec',\n    '            h = torch.tanh(a_t)',\n    '            y_t = h @ W_out.T + b_out',\n    '            hs.append(h)',\n    '            as_.append(a_t)',\n    '            outputs.append(y_t)',\n    '        outputs_t = torch.stack(outputs, dim=1)',\n    '        # Compute output errors',\n    '        out_err = (outputs_t - targets) * output_mask.unsqueeze(-1)',\n    '        # Backward pass using FA feedback matrices',\n    '        # Accumulate gradients',\n    '        dW_in  = torch.zeros_like(W_in)',\n    '        dW_rec = torch.zeros_like(W_rec)',\n    '        db_rec = torch.zeros_like(b_rec)',\n    '        dW_out = torch.zeros_like(W_out)',\n    '        db_out = torch.zeros_like(b_out)',\n    '        delta_h_next = torch.zeros(batch_size, HIDDEN_SIZE, device=device)',\n    '        for t in reversed(range(SEQ_LEN)):',\n    '            h_t = hs[t]',\n    '            h_prev = hs[t-1] if t > 0 else torch.zeros(batch_size, HIDDEN_SIZE, device=device)',\n    '            x_t = inputs[:, t, :]',\n    '            dtanh = 1.0 - h_t**2',\n    '            # Output error projected back via B_out (FA) instead of W_out^T',\n    '            e_out_t = out_err[:, t, :]  # (batch, output_dim)',\n    '            delta_out = e_out_t @ B_out.T  # (batch, hidden) - FA feedback',\n    '            # Total delta_h: from output error + recurrent from next step via B_rec',\n    '            delta_h = (delta_out + delta_h_next) * dtanh',\n    '            dW_out += e_out_t.T @ h_t / batch_size',\n    '            db_out += e_out_t.mean(0)',\n    '            dW_rec += delta_h.T @ h_prev / batch_size',\n    '            dW_in  += delta_h.T @ x_t / batch_size',\n    '            db_rec += delta_h.mean(0)',\n    '            # Propagate delta_h to next step via B_rec (FA)',\n    '            delta_h_next = delta_h @ B_rec.T',\n    '        # Clip gradients',\n    '        grads = [dW_in, dW_rec, db_rec, dW_out, db_out]',\n    '        total_norm = sum(g.norm()**2 for g in grads)**0.5',\n    '        clip = 1.0 / (total_norm + 1e-8)',\n    '        if total_norm > 1.0:',\n    '            grads = [g * clip for g in grads]',\n    '        # Adam update',\n    '        t_adam = it + 1',\n    '        for i, (p, g) in enumerate(zip(params, grads)):',\n    '            m[i] = beta1*m[i] + (1-beta1)*g',\n    '            v[i] = beta2*v[i] + (1-beta2)*g**2',\n    '            m_hat = m[i] / (1 - beta1**t_adam)',\n    '            v_hat = v[i] / (1 - beta2**t_adam)',\n    '            p.data -= lr * m_hat / (v_hat.sqrt() + eps)',\n    '        mse, acc = compute_metrics(outputs_t.detach(), targets, output_mask)',\n    '        mse_history.append(mse)',\n    '        acc_history.append(acc)',\n    '        if verbose and (it+1) % log_interval == 0:',\n    '            print(\"  Iter\", it+1, \"| MSE:\", round(mse, 5), \"| Acc:\", round(acc, 4))',\n    '    return mse_history, acc_history',\n    '',\n    'print(\"\")',\n    'print(\"=== Testing FA (200 iterations, lr=0.001) ===\")',\n    'mse_h, acc_h = train_fa(n_iter=200, lr=0.001, log_interval=50)',\n    'print(\"\")',\n    'print(\"FA Quick Test Results:\")',\n    'print(\"  Initial MSE:\", round(mse_h[0], 5))',\n    'print(\"  Final MSE:\", round(mse_h[-1], 5))',\n    'print(\"  Final Acc:\", round(acc_h[-1], 4))',\n    'print(\"  Learning:\", mse_h[-1] < mse_h[0])',\n    '',\n    'print(\"\")',\n    'print(\"=== Testing FA (500 iterations, lr=0.001) ===\")',\n    'mse_h2, acc_h2 = train_fa(n_iter=500, lr=0.001, log_interval=100)',\n    'print(\"\")',\n    'print(\"FA 500-iter Results:\")',\n    'print(\"  Initial MSE:\", round(mse_h2[0], 5))',\n    'print(\"  Final MSE:\", round(mse_h2[-1], 5))',\n    'print(\"  Final Acc:\", round(acc_h2[-1], 4))',\n    '',\n    'print(\"\")',\n    'print(\"=== Step 3: FA Implementation VERIFIED ===\")',\n]\n\nwith open(script_path, 'w') as f:\n    f.write('\\n'.join(lines))\n\nresult = subprocess.run([PYTHON, script_path], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=180)\nprint('STDOUT:', result.stdout)\nif result.stderr:\n    print('STDERR:', result.stderr[:2000])\nprint('Return code:', result.returncode)\n"}

import os

PYTHON = '/home/zihan.zhang/.conda/envs/panda/bin/python'
